For the disc triangle, the centroid lies inside the rim where ρ < 7, so s(centroid) = ρ_c − R < 0, which is correct. I'll create a helper to compute the face centroid and evaluate the locus there, then replace all the `face_mean` calls with this geometrically meaningful approach for consistency.


## 🤖🔧 Assistant
<details>
<summary>Bash: Use the true centroid for side evaluation</summary>

```bash
python3 - <<'EOF'
p='src/stage2/locus.rs'
s=open(p).read()

# Replace the vertex-mean helper with a true centroid, and evaluate the locus there.
s=s.replace('''/// Mean of `f` over a face's vertices.
pub(crate) fn face_mean(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, fi: usize, f: impl Fn(&[f64; 3]) -> f64) -> f64 {
    let face = &mesh.faces[fi];
    let vc = face.vertex_count as usize;
    let mut sum = 0.0;
    for &vi in &face.vertex_indices[..vc] {
        sum += f(&point_of(&mesh.vertices[vi]));
    }
    sum / vc as f64
}''','''/// A face's centroid.
///
/// The locus is evaluated here rather than averaged over the vertices: `side`
/// is not affine in position (the meridian radius is not), and the difference
/// is exactly what matters for a face whose vertices all sit *on* the junction.
fn face_centroid(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, fi: usize) -> [f64; 3] {
    let face = &mesh.faces[fi];
    let vc = face.vertex_count as usize;
    let mut c = [0.0; 3];
    for &vi in &face.vertex_indices[..vc] {
        c = add(c, point_of(&mesh.vertices[vi]));
    }
    scale(c, 1.0 / vc as f64)
}

/// Which side of the locus a face's interior lies on.
fn face_side(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, fi: usize, locus: &Locus) -> f64 {
    locus.side(&face_centroid(mesh, fi))
}''')

s=s.replace('''            .map(|&fi| face_mean(mesh, fi, |p| locus.side(p)).signum())''','''            .map(|&fi| face_side(mesh, fi, locus).signum())''')
s=s.replace('''        let s = face_mean(mesh, fi, |p| locus.side(p));''','''        let s = face_side(mesh, fi, locus);''')
open(p,'w').write(s)
print(s.count('face_mean'), "face_mean refs left")
EOF

/// Repair-operation counts, for logging.

pub(super) struct RepairStats {
    pub snapped: usize,
    pub snap_problems: usize,
    pub relabeled: usize,
    pub merged: usize,
    pub split: usize,
}

/// Run the stage-2.4 repair sequence in place, then assert the hard
/// partition invariants.
pub(super) fn repair(mesh: &ConnectedMesh, cls: &mut Classification, config: &Config) -> RepairStats {
    let centroids: Vec<[f64; 3]> = mesh
        .faces
        .iter()
        .map(|f| face_centroid(f, &mesh.vertices))
        .collect();

    let (snapped, snap_problems) = snap_boundaries(mesh, cls, config);
    let stats = RepairStats {
        snapped,
        snap_problems,
        relabeled: relabel_boundaries(mesh, cls, config),
        merged: merge_regions(mesh, cls, &centroids, config),
        split: resplit_disconnected(mesh, cls),
    };

**After:**
```
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Boundary snap
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Put every shared boundary on the mesh-edge chain the junction geometry
/// picks out, rather than wherever flood and claim resolution happened to
/// leave it.
///
/// For each adjacent pair of committed surfaces, `locus.rs` builds the curve
/// where they meet and turns it into a signed side test; a face on the wrong
/// side, that the other surface also fits, moves. This is a *selection*, not a
/// search: the seam assumption guarantees the correct boundary already exists
/// as a chain of mesh edges, so the only question is which one.
///
/// Assignment and fit are one problem (the same lesson `relabel_boundaries`
/// records), so each round refits every surface its moves touched and rebuilds
/// the loci from the corrected geometry. A face may move at most once per
/// invocation, which bounds the alternation without a round cap: the locus
/// each round is computed from strictly better-supported fits, so a face that
/// wants to move back is evidence the locus was wrong rather than a step to
/// iterate on.
///
/// Pairs the module cannot build a locus for, or whose locus fails validation,
/// are **reported and left alone**. Transversal junctions are not snap's
/// business at all — they are sharp mesh edges, which stage 2.1 already
/// partitions exactly.
fn snap_boundaries(
    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
    cls: &mut Classification,
    config: &Config,
) -> (usize, usize) {
    let mut already_moved = vec![false; mesh.faces.len()];
    let mut total = 0usize;
    let mut reported: BTreeSet<String> = BTreeSet::new();

    loop {
        let f2s = face_to_surface(mesh, cls);
        let surfs: Vec<Surf<'_>> = cls
            .selected
            .iter()
            .map(|s| Surf::of(s, &cls.planar, &cls.conical, &cls.spherical, &cls.toroidal))
            .collect();
        let pairs = analyze_pairs(mesh, &surfs, &f2s, config.vertex_tolerance_mm);

        let mut round: Vec<(usize, usize, usize)> = Vec::new();
        let mut claimed = vec![false; mesh.faces.len()];
        for p in &pairs {
            if p.is_problem() {
                reported.insert(format!(
                    "  Stage 2.4 snap: surfaces {} ({}) and {} ({}), {} interface faces — {}",
                    p.a, p.kind_a, p.b, p.kind_b, p.interface_faces, p.describe(),
                ));
                continue;
            }
            if let PairOutcome::Snapped { moves, .. } = &p.outcome {
                for &(fi, from, to) in moves {
                    // One move per face per invocation, and one claimant per
                    // face per round: a face at a triple point borders two
                    // junctions, and the first pair in index order takes it.
                    if !already_moved[fi] && !claimed[fi] {
                        claimed[fi] = true;
                        round.push((fi, from, to));
                    }
                }
            }
        }

        if round.is_empty() {
            for line in &reported {
                eprintln!("{line}");
            }
            return (total, reported.len());
        }

        let mut touched: BTreeSet<usize> = BTreeSet::new();
        for &(fi, from, _) in &round {
            surface_faces_mut(cls, from).retain(|&f| f != fi);
            touched.insert(from);
        }
        for &(fi, _, to) in &round {
            surface_faces_mut(cls, to).push(fi);
            touched.insert(to);
            already_moved[fi] = true;
        }
        for si in touched {
            refit_geometry(mesh, cls, si);
        }
        total += round.len();
    }
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Boundary relabel (port of the old refine_boundary_assignments)
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
```

<details>

**Before:**

/// Compare stage 3.1 adjacency graph against reference STEP shape.
```

**After:**
```
/// Name an edge by the two surfaces it joins, whether it is tangent, and where
/// its worst boundary vertex sits — enough to take straight to
/// `dump_regions at <x> <y> <z>` or `dump_regions locus`.
fn describe_edge(
    ei: usize,
    worst_vi: usize,
    edges: &[ReconEdge],
    face_descriptors: &[FaceDescriptor],
    stage2: &Stage2Output,
) -> String {
    let kind = |fi: usize| -> String {
        let si = face_descriptors[fi].selected_surface_idx;
        let name = match stage2.selected_surfaces[si] {
            SelectedSurface::Planar(_) => "plane",
            SelectedSurface::Conical(i) => {
                if stage2.conical_hypotheses[i].is_cylinder() {
                    "cyl"
                } else {
                    "cone"
                }
            }
            SelectedSurface::Spherical(_) => "sphere",
            SelectedSurface::Toroidal(_) => "torus",
            SelectedSurface::BSpline(_) => "bspline",
        };
        format!("{si} {name}")
    };
    let edge = &edges[ei];
    let [f0, f1] = edge.face_indices;
    let v = &stage2.mesh.vertices[worst_vi];
    format!(
        "edge {ei} ({} | {}){}, worst boundary vertex {worst_vi} at ({:.4}, {:.4}, {:.4})",
        kind(f0),
        kind(f1),
        if edge.tangent { " tangent" } else { "" },
        v.x,
        v.y,
        v.z,
    )
}

/// Compare stage 3.1 adjacency graph against reference STEP shape.

/// Per-face detail for one pair's interface: why each face does or does not
/// move. Diagnostic only.
pub(crate) fn explain_pair(
    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
    surfs: &[Surf<'_>],
    f2s: &[usize],
    vtol: f64,
    a: usize,
    b: usize,
) -> Vec<String> {
    let mut counts = vec![0usize; surfs.len()];
    for &si in f2s {
        if si != usize::MAX {
            counts[si] += 1;
        }
    }
    let Some((_, _, interface)) = interfaces(mesh, f2s)
        .into_iter()
        .find(|&(x, y, _)| (x, y) == (a.min(b), a.max(b)))
    else {
        return vec![format!("surfaces {a} and {b} do not share a boundary")];
    };
    let (a, b) = (a.min(b), a.max(b));
    let analysis = analyze_pair(mesh, surfs, f2s, &counts, a, b, &interface, vtol);
    let mut out = vec![
        format!(
            "pair {a} ({}) | {b} ({}): {}",
            surfs[a].kind(),
            surfs[b].kind(),
            analysis.describe()
        ),
        format!(
            "  {} faces on {a}'s side, {} on {b}'s; {} chain vertices",
            interface.faces_a.len(),
            interface.faces_b.len(),
            interface.vertices.len(),
        ),
    ];
    let PairOutcome::Snapped { locus, moves, .. } = &analysis.outcome else {
        return out;
    };
    let moving: std::collections::BTreeSet<usize> = moves.iter().map(|m| m.0).collect();
    out.push(
        "  face      owner  diameter    s(centroid)   s(min)     s(max)  fits:a b  move".to_string(),
    );
    for &fi in interface.faces_a.iter().chain(interface.faces_b.iter()) {
        let face = &mesh.faces[fi];
        let vc = face.vertex_count as usize;
        let sv: Vec<f64> = face.vertex_indices[..vc]
            .iter()
            .map(|&vi| locus.side(&point_of(&mesh.vertices[vi])))
            .collect();
        out.push(format!(
            "  {:>7}  {:>5}  {:>9.3e}  {:>11.3e}  {:>9.2e}  {:>9.2e}   {} {}   {}",
            fi,
            f2s[fi],
            face_diameter(mesh, fi),
            face_side(mesh, fi, locus),
            sv.iter().cloned().fold(f64::INFINITY, f64::min),
            sv.iter().cloned().fold(f64::NEG_INFINITY, f64::max),
            if face_fits(mesh, surfs[a], fi, vtol) { "Y" } else { "." },
            if face_fits(mesh, surfs[b], fi, vtol) { "Y" } else { "." },
            if moving.contains(&fi) { "YES" } else { "" },
        ));
    }
    out
}

/// The faces on each side of every surface-to-surface interface, keyed by the
/// ordered pair (a < b) of selected-surface indices.
```
</details>

by region/hypothesis index, smallest region first for merges — bounded
rounds).

**Claim resolution** — the boundary placement at tangent junctions: faces
claimed by multiple hypotheses (junction rows) go to the claimant whose
surface fits their vertices best, requiring a **2× margin** to prefer a
different side than the larger claimant (a true junction face fits both
sides equally — the margin prevents arbitrary flapping; residual ties
break to the larger claimant, then lower hypothesis index). Exact vertex
distances, no curvature involved.

**Boundary relabel** (±1-row polish; port of the old
\`refine_boundary_assignments\`): after resolution, walk region-boundary
faces; move a face when an adjacent region's surface fits its vertices at
least 2× better; iterate to a fixpoint (≤ 10 rounds; boundaries only move
locally), then rebuild affected regions' vertex lists. Region *geometry*
is not refitted — only the membership map changes.

**Merge**: absorb region S into an adjacent larger region T when every
vertex of S lies within \`--vertex-tolerance\` of T's fitted surface and
every centroid of S within \`--surface-tolerance\`; refit the union,
revalidate, keep only if valid. Eats junction-circle singletons (faces
whose vertices sit ON a tangent seam — genuinely ambiguous, claimable by
either side), chord-degenerate slivers (a single-facet-wide band of a
coarse cylinder validates as its own facet plane, but its vertices lie
exactly on the neighbor cylinder), and residual same-surface fragments.
This is the abandoned absorb pass's *tight* rule reborn with a principled
criterion — vertex tolerance, at which "on the same surface" is decisive;
never the chordal slop of the abandoned loose rule (0.4mm slop, 794
BRepCheck failures on planar-only 3DBenchy). B-spline surfaces are never
merge TARGETS for faces outside their fitted region: a fit vouches only
for its data (the island-dissolution guard in 2.3(e) is the fit-validated
way into a freeform patch).

**Connectivity re-split + invariants** (hard assertions before stage 3):
resolution and relabeling can disconnect a region — re-split into
mesh-connected components (identical fitted geometry on two non-adjacent
regions is legal). Then assert: every mesh face belongs to exactly one
region (total coverage); every region is mesh-connected; every region's
surface validates the region's own vertices/centroids.
```

- *Model selection* — which candidate wins. This is the only one that should be
  a comparison, and it is the one that most often gets written as a threshold
  when it should not be.

**Calibrate acceptance on the fit, not on a constant.** Whether a face belongs
to a surface is decided at a tolerance, and a single absolute tolerance cannot
be right: it has to be loose enough for the worst-conditioned fit in the corpus,
which leaves it hundreds of times too loose for a converged one. The quantities
involved are not comparable. A mesh vertex lies on the true CAD surface to
~1e-6 mm (measured across the corpus, coarse tessellations included), so a face
that really belongs misses its fit by the *fit's* error alone, while a face that
does not belong misses it by a geometric departure — one tessellation row past a
tangent junction sits ~h²/2r away, which shrinks quadratically as the mesh
refines. So the flood scales its bar by the fit's own robust residual scale
(\`acceptance_tol\`, compete.rs): a fit still bootstrapping off a partial patch
reports a large scale and keeps the room it needs, and the bar tightens by
itself as the support fills in. The scale is a *median*, not an RMS, because the
faces it exists to detect are sitting in the sample and would inflate an RMS.

Two properties are worth keeping: the criterion carries no length, so it needs
no recalibration per model, feature size or tessellation density; and it is
insensitive to its own parameter — the corpus gives identical decompositions at
5, 8 and 15 σ, where the absolute bar it replaces went from correct to total
collapse over a 3.3× change. Bounds remain: the floor is \`--vertex-tolerance\`,
below which no distinction is claimed, and the ceiling is the old fixed bar, so
the criterion can only ever be *tighter* than the previous behaviour. The
ceiling is load-bearing — removing it lets a poor fit report a large scale,
admit more foreign faces and inflate its scale further.

**The jet is a prior, not a gate.** Stage 2.3(b) states this — validation
decides every face — but \`seed_patch\` was gating its BFS on jet similarity, and
on a small fillet tessellated into a handful of facets the jet is not reliable
enough to gate on: adjacent facets of one exact r = 0.5 arc read κ₁ = 0.023,
0.162 and 2.2. The filter starved the patch below what a 5-DOF fit needs, so
every candidate failed and the arc fell to one plane per facet. It now falls

```

**After:**
```markdown
**Snap shared boundaries to the junction, not to a residual comparison.**
Nothing in stage 2 reasoned about where a boundary *should* be — it fell out of
flood plus claim resolution, and every remaining `--compare` failure has been a
boundary sitting a tessellation row off. The seam assumption says the correct
boundary already exists in the mesh as a chain of edges, so this is selection
from a guaranteed-nonempty set rather than a search. `locus.rs` supplies the
criterion and `snap_boundaries` (repair.rs) applies it.

*Use the minimum-distance locus, not the intersection.* At a tangent junction
the surfaces graze rather than cross, and their intersection is catastrophically
ill-conditioned: a plane offset by δ from the torus it should graze meets it in
a circle displaced by √(2rδ), which is how a 4.7e-4 mm plane-fit error became
the recorded 4.4e-2 mm edge error. The locus of minimum distance — the set of
points where the two normal fields agree — has no such amplification, and the
reason is worth remembering: displacing the plane along its own normal leaves
the tangency circle exactly where it was, so the locus does not depend on the
quantity that gets amplified at all. Only a *direction* error moves it, and
linearly. Positions depart quadratically from a tangency while normals turn
linearly; key the boundary off the latter. (This is verified by unit test in
`locus.rs`, not just asserted.)

*The meridian reduction.* Nearly every tangent junction in CAD joins two
surfaces of revolution about a common axis. Reduce the pair to the meridian
half-plane (ρ, h) of that axis, where each surface is a line (plane ⊥ axis,
cylinder, cone) or a circle (sphere, torus) and coaxial tangency is exactly
meridian tangency; the junction is the circle swept by one meridian point q,
and the side test is displacement along the curves' common tangent there. This
subsumes the cases one would otherwise write out separately, and explains why:
at a plane-torus junction ρ is the informative coordinate and h is stationary,
at a cylinder-torus junction it is the other way round, so neither coordinate
works alone and the common tangent is the thing to project onto. Two junctions
are not axisymmetric and are built directly: a plane grazing a cylinder along a
ruling, and the tube junction where a straight fillet (cylinder) continues into
a corner fillet (torus) — there the axes are *perpendicular*, the surfaces share
the tube rather than an axis, and C¹ continuity forces the cylinder's axis to be
tangent to the torus's spine circle, so the junction is the foot of the
perpendicular from the torus centre onto that axis. Note that neither fit says
where its own surface *ends*; the junction has to be read off the pair.

*What makes a move safe.* Three conditions together, none of them a tuned
number: the surface a face would move to must actually fit it (soundness — a
boundary correction must never hand a face to a surface that does not explain
it, and this is what stops a large fused planar quad from being swallowed by a
fillet it happens to touch); the locus must put the whole face on that side,
*including its interior* — a disc triangulated with every vertex on its rim has
all its corners sitting on the junction and on both surfaces at once, so the
corners decide nothing and the centroid must; and the moved set must be
reachable from the current boundary through other moving faces. Orientation —
which side belongs to which surface — cannot be read off the supports globally,
because a locus circle is complete while the junction is only the arc where the
surfaces meet, so a large planar face wanders to the far side of its own
tangency circle while remaining unambiguously the plane's. Read it off the
finely tessellated side, whose extent is comparable to the junction, and only
from faces the other surface cannot explain.

*Loud, not lenient.* A tangent pair whose locus cannot be built or does not
validate is reported and left alone; transversal junctions are out of scope by
construction, being sharp mesh edges that 2.1 already partitions exactly. The
report is what found the rim-triangulated-disc case above.

*What it retired, and honest accounting.* Relabel's **2× anti-flapping margin**
and **10-round cap** are gone. Both were the price of a criterion that compares
a face against two fits *each contaminated by that face*: it could oscillate, so
the margin held it back and the cap stopped it. In-loop refitting removed the
contamination, and allowing each face to move at most once bounds the
alternation as a statement about convergence rather than a tuned number. Both
were inline literals, so the stage-2 constant count does not fall; `locus.rs`
adds two (`COAXIAL_TOL`, a validity precondition on the meridian reduction, and
`TANGENCY_TOL_RAD`, which is stage 3.2's existing tangency criterion given one
definition shared by both stages), taking the grep count 35 → 37.
**Measured: with the margin and cap gone, snap moves zero faces on every corpus
model — unconstrained relabel now reaches the same boundary on its own, and the
suite passes with snap ablated.** So snap is currently a correctness *guarantee*
rather than a correctness *fix*: the boundaries relabel converges to are the
ones the junction geometry demands, which the `dump_regions locus` diagnostic
confirms corpus-wide (zero wrong-side faces). Recorded plainly so the next
session can decide whether an inert pass earns its place; it is also the exact
per-face assignment criterion item C (variational shape approximation) needs.

**The jet is a prior, not a gate.** Stage 2.3(b) states this — validation
decides every face — but `seed_patch` was gating its BFS on jet similarity, and
```

+// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
+// Boundary snap
+// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
+
+/// Put every shared boundary on the mesh-edge chain the junction geometry
+/// picks out, rather than wherever flood and claim resolution happened to
+/// leave it.
+///
+/// For each adjacent pair of committed surfaces, \`locus.rs\` builds the curve
+/// where they meet and turns it into a signed side test; a face on the wrong
+/// side, that the other surface also fits, moves. This is a *selection*, not a
+/// search: the seam assumption guarantees the correct boundary already exists
+/// as a chain of mesh edges, so the only question is which one.
+///
+/// Assignment and fit are one problem (the same lesson \`relabel_boundaries\`
+/// records), so each round refits every surface its moves touched and rebuilds
+/// the loci from the corrected geometry. A face may move at most once per
+/// invocation, which bounds the alternation without a round cap: the locus
+/// each round is computed from strictly better-supported fits, so a face that
+/// wants to move back is evidence the locus was wrong rather than a step to
+/// iterate on.
+///
+/// Pairs the module cannot build a locus for, or whose locus fails validation,
+/// are **reported and left alone**. Transversal junctions are not snap's
+/// business at all — they are sharp mesh edges, which stage 2.1 already
+/// partitions exactly.
+fn snap_boundaries(
+    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
+    cls: &mut Classification,
+    config: &Config,
+) -> (usize, usize) {
+    let mut already_moved = vec![false; mesh.faces.len()];
+    let mut total = 0usize;
+    let mut reported: BTreeSet<String> = BTreeSet::new();
+
+    loop {
+        let f2s = face_to_surface(mesh, cls);
+        let surfs: Vec<Surf<'_>> = cls
+            .selected
+            .iter()
+            .map(|s| Surf::of(s, &cls.planar, &cls.conical, &cls.spherical, &cls.toroidal))
+            .collect();
+        let pairs = analyze_pairs(mesh, &surfs, &f2s, config.vertex_tolerance_mm);
+
+        let mut round: Vec<(usize, usize, usize)> = Vec::new();
+        let mut claimed = vec![false; mesh.faces.len()];
+        for p in &pairs {
+            if p.is_problem() {
+                reported.insert(format!(
+                    "  Stage 2.4 snap: surfaces {} ({}) and {} ({}), {} interface faces — {}",
+                    p.a, p.kind_a, p.b, p.kind_b, p.interface_faces, p.describe(),
+                ));
+                continue;
+            }
+            if let PairOutcome::Snapped { moves, .. } = &p.outcome {
+                for &(fi, from, to) in moves {
+                    // One move per face per invocation, and one claimant per
+                    // face per round: a face at a triple point borders two
+                    // junctions, and the first pair in index order takes it.
+                    if !already_moved[fi] && !claimed[fi] {
+                        claimed[fi] = true;
+                        round.push((fi, from, to));
```

**After:**
```
/// How many adjacent surface pairs stage 2.4's boundary snap could not handle,
/// and how many faces are still on the wrong side of a junction it could.
///
/// Both should be zero on a well-decomposed model: the second is the property
/// snap exists to establish, and the first is the set of junction geometries
/// `locus.rs` does not implement. Exposed so tests can assert on it.
pub fn locus_summary(out: &Stage2Output, config: &Config) -> (usize, usize) {
    let (surfs, f2s) = locus_inputs(out);
    let pairs = locus::analyze_pairs(&out.mesh, &surfs, &f2s, config.vertex_tolerance_mm);
    let problems = pairs.iter().filter(|p| p.is_problem()).count();
    let wrong_side = pairs
        .iter()
        .map(|p| match &p.outcome {
            locus::PairOutcome::Snapped { moves, .. } => moves.len(),
            _ => 0,
        })
        .sum();
    (problems, wrong_side)
}

/// Diagnostic: per-face detail for one pair's shared boundary — which faces
/// the locus would move and, for the ones it would not, which test stopped it.

//! Integration tests for stage 2.3(b) scoped fit competition: tangent-chain
//! regions the whole-region ladder cannot explain must decompose into their
//! true analytic surfaces (vertex-tolerance validated, not just counted).
//!
//! These run the full stage-2 pipeline (default stage) and assert the typed
//! hypothesis counts on the headline acceptance models, including the
//! stage-2.3(c) bent-tube promotion win (pipe elbow → torus + 2 cylinders).

use brepper::config;
use brepper::{stage1, stage2};

fn manifest_dir() -> String {
    env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR").to_string()
}

fn run(stl_rel: &str) -> stage2::Stage2Output {
    let stl = format!("{}/{stl_rel}", manifest_dir());
    let config = config::parse_config_from(["brepper", &stl, "-q"]).unwrap();
    let mesh = stage1::stage1(&config).expect("stage1");
    stage2::stage2(&config, mesh, None).expect("stage2")
}

/// Count selected surfaces by type: (planar, cylinder, cone, spherical,
/// toroidal, bspline). Per project policy, assertions are on stage 2.6
/// *selected* surfaces — the hypothesis vectors also carry entries that repair
/// emptied out.
fn selected_counts(out: &stage2::Stage2Output) -> [usize; 6] {
    let mut n = [0usize; 6];
    for s in &out.selected_surfaces {
        match s {
            stage2::SelectedSurface::Planar(_) => n[0] += 1,
            stage2::SelectedSurface::Conical(i) => {
                if out.conical_hypotheses[*i].is_cylinder() {
                    n[1] += 1
                } else {
                    n[2] += 1
                }
            }
            stage2::SelectedSurface::Spherical(_) => n[3] += 1,
            stage2::SelectedSurface::Toroidal(_) => n[4] += 1,
```

> The final state shows zero unhandled pairs because the problematic pair gets absorbed during the merge phase, even though it exists when the snap runs. So I should update the test expectations to all zeros and simplify it. But there's a complication — the loud warning message fires for a pair that merge subsequently handles, which creates a timing issue.
> 
> The real question is ordering: should snap run before or after merge? If I move snap to after merge and resplit, the pair won't exist when snap checks, so there's no spurious complaint. But merge might create new boundaries that need snapping, and snap can disconnect surfaces, so resplit needs to come after. Let me try the sequence relabel → merge → resplit → snap instead — that way merge's dissolution happens before snap reports anything, and resplit still cleans up after snap. This should reduce the noise from false positives while keeping the criterion's final say.

<summary>Bash: Commit the work</summary>

```bash
git commit -F - <<'EOF'
Snap tangent boundaries to their junction; retire relabel's margin and cap

Stage 2 had nothing that reasoned about where a shared boundary *should*
be — it fell out of flood plus claim resolution, and every remaining
--compare failure has been a boundary a tessellation row off. The seam
assumption says the correct boundary already exists in the mesh as a
chain of edges, so this is selection from a guaranteed-nonempty set, not
a search. New `src/stage2/locus.rs` supplies the criterion; new
`snap_boundaries` (repair.rs, after relabel and merge) applies it.

Use the minimum-distance locus, never the intersection. At a tangent
junction the surfaces graze, and a plane offset by δ from the torus it
should graze meets it in a circle displaced by √(2rδ) — the recorded
4.7e-4 mm plane error becoming a 4.4e-2 mm edge error. The tangency locus
does not depend on that offset at all: displacing the plane along its own
normal leaves the circle exactly where it was. Positions depart
quadratically from a tangency, normals turn linearly; key off the latter.
Unit-tested, not just asserted.

Most tangent junctions are coaxial surfaces of revolution, so reduce the
pair to the meridian half-plane, where each is a line or a circle and
tangency is meridian tangency; the side test is displacement along the
common tangent. That subsumes the per-type cases and shows why neither ρ
nor h works alone — at each junction one of them is stationary. Two
junctions are built directly: a plane grazing a cylinder along a ruling,
and the tube junction where a straight fillet continues into a corner
fillet (axes perpendicular, junction at the foot of the perpendicular
from the torus centre onto the cylinder axis).

A face moves only if the target surface actually fits it, the locus puts
its *interior* on that side, and it is reachable from the current
boundary. The interior test is load-bearing: a disc triangulated with
every vertex on its rim has all its corners on the junction and on both
surfaces, so the corners decide nothing — found by the loud reporting on
filleted_cylinder. Pairs with no locus, or a locus that fails validation,
are reported and left alone; transversal junctions are out of scope,
being sharp edges 2.1 already partitions exactly.

Retires relabel's 2× anti-flapping margin and its 10-round cap, as
planned. Both were the price of comparing a face against two fits each
contaminated by that face; in-loop refitting removed the contamination,
and letting each face move at most once bounds the loop as a convergence
statement rather than a number.

Honest accounting: both were inline literals, so the stage-2 constant
count does not fall — locus.rs adds two (COAXIAL_TOL; TANGENCY_TOL_RAD,
which gives stage 3.2's existing tangency criterion one shared
definition), taking the grep count 35 → 37. And with the margin and cap
gone, snap moves zero faces on every corpus model: unconstrained relabel
now reaches the same boundary itself, and the suite passes with snap
ablated. It is a correctness guarantee rather than a fix today. Recorded
in DEVELOPMENT_PLAN so the next session can decide whether an inert pass
earns its place.

Diagnostics: `dump_regions locus [a b]` reports per pair what the
junction geometry concludes, with per-face detail for one pair; stage
3.1's --compare now names its worst edge by both surfaces and tangency
even when it passes. New `boundaries_sit_on_their_junctions` pins zero
wrong-side faces and zero unhandled junctions across seven models — a
sharper guard than surface counts, which a one-row error leaves intact.

Suite 509 passing / 3 ignored, clippy clean, corpus decomposition
identical (tmp/probe.sh). stepped_block fine still passes at 4.36e-2 mm;
its worst edge is a torus↔cylinder tube junction whose boundary sits on
the locus to ~1e-6 mm, so that residual is not a wrong-side-face problem.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
EOF

Positions depart quadratically from a tangency while normals turn linearly.** **THE MERIDIAN REDUCTION (the unifying idea): most tangent junctions join coaxial surfaces of revolution; reduce to the (ρ,h) half-plane where each is a LINE (plane⊥axis, cylinder, cone) or a CIRCLE (sphere, torus), coaxial tangency = meridian tangency, and the side test is displacement along the common tangent at the tangency point. This subsumes the per-type cases AND explains why neither coordinate works alone — at a plane/torus junction ρ is informative and h stationary, at a cylinder/torus junction vice versa.** Two junctions are NOT axisymmetric and are built directly: plane-grazing-cylinder along a ruling, and the **TUBE JUNCTION** where a straight fillet (cylinder) continues into a corner fillet (torus) — **axes PERPENDICULAR, they share the tube not an axis; C¹ forces the cylinder axis to be tangent to the torus spine circle, so the junction is the FOOT OF THE PERPENDICULAR from the torus centre onto the cylinder axis.** That case was 36 of the pairs on stepped_block fine and is invisible to a coaxial-only implementation. **A MOVE NEEDS THREE THINGS, none of them tuned: (1) the target surface actually FITS the face (soundness — this is what stops a big fused planar quad being swallowed by a fillet it touches); (2) the locus puts the face's INTERIOR on that side — the CENTROID, not just the vertices, because a disc triangulated with every vertex on its rim has all corners ON the junction and on BOTH surfaces (found by the loud reporting on filleted_cylinder, a real bug); (3) reachable from the current boundary through other moving faces.** **ORIENTATION IS THE SUBTLE PART: a locus circle is COMPLETE but the junction is only the ARC where the surfaces meet, so a large planar face wanders to the far side of its own tangency circle while remaining unambiguously the plane's — you CANNOT orient from the supports globally. Read it off the FINELY TESSELLATED side (extent comparable to the junction), and only from its faces the other surface cannot explain.** **WHAT IT RETIRED: relabel's 2× anti-flapping margin and 10-round cap, both DELETED** (they were the price of comparing a face against two fits each contaminated by that face; in-loop refitting removed the contamination and "each face moves at most once" bounds the loop as a convergence statement). **HONEST ACCOUNTING — READ THIS BEFORE VALUING THE WORK: (a) both were INLINE LITERALS so the constant count does NOT fall; locus.rs adds 2 (`COAXIAL_TOL`, `TANGENCY_TOL_RAD`), grep count 35 → 37. Medium's worst edge is torus↔BSPLINE, i.e. the known decomposition gap (81 surfaces vs 71), not boundary placement.** **NEW DIAGNOSTICS, USE THEM FIRST: `dump_regions <stl> locus` = per adjacent surface pair, tangent/transversal + locus built or not + how far the locus sits from the current chain + faces on the wrong side, problems first; `dump_regions <stl> locus <a> <b>` = per-face table (owner, diameter, s(centroid), s(min/max), fits-a/fits-b, would-move). Stage 3.1's `--compare` now NAMES ITS WORST EDGE by both surfaces and tangency EVEN WHEN IT PASSES (`worst edge edge 71 (10 torus | 33 cyl) tangent, worst boundary vertex ... at (x,y,z)`) — this is how the tube junction was identified in one run.** **NEW REGRESSION GUARD: `boundaries_sit_on_their_junctions` (tests/stage2_competition.rs) asserts ZERO wrong-side faces AND ZERO unhandled junctions across 7 models. Much sharper than surface counts, which a one-row boundary error leaves completely intact.** Still unhandled (reported loudly, nothing in the corpus fires): cone-plane ruling tangency, non-coaxial pairs other than the tube junction (notably torus↔torus), point tangencies. **NEXT = TODO item B (estimate `--surface-tolerance`/`--angular-tolerance` from the mesh).**

- **NEXT-WORK PLAN IN TODO (2026-07-28, commit 1950609, docs only). Item A now DONE (above); B and C still open.** **CORE INSIGHT: stage 2 did NOT use the seam assumption at all — it is cited as the reason for partition-then-classify but nothing found seams or required boundaries to look like them. Stage 3 DOES use it (§3.x shared-edge topological assembly) and that DELETED geometric sewing outright — the proof of concept. THE PRIZE: ~2/3 of stage 2's thresholds exist ONLY because tangent seams are invisible; if the partition were known, `classify_one_region` (already written, constants are cost-control not decisions) does the job — evidenced by sharp-edged models passing the ladder with 0 competition regions and no tuning.** **(B) estimate `--surface-tolerance` and `--angular-tolerance` FROM THE MESH** — both are stand-ins for EXPORTER settings, not fitting properties (`--vertex-tolerance` is a genuine user request, keep it a flag). Measurable without a reference because **the mesh is its own reference: vertices lie on the true surface (~1e-6mm), centroids sit inside by the chordal sag**. `depth ≈ ρ²/(2R)`; fit-free `sag ≈ dihedral × facet size / 8`. **The 0.4mm default is ~400× too loose — true sag is 8.9e-4 (stepped_block fine) / 9.5e-4 (rounded_cube fine), from stage-1 --compare.** Reference STEPs = VALIDATION INSTRUMENT, never production input. Angular tolerance = find the GAP in the bimodal dihedral distribution (Otsu) instead of fixing 25°; that also gives the OpenSCAD detector free. **(C) reformulate competition as alternating partition/fit = Variational Shape Approximation / Lloyd** — half of it already works here (relabel+refit converged, stepped_block fine 73→71 surfaces). Do C LAST and only after the sweep harness; snap's per-face criterion is what C needs. **THEN: a documented ablation protocol for deleting what's left, most-likely-dead first (MIN_AXIAL_SWEEP_DEG, MAX_TORUS_RADIUS_RATIO, LEFTOVER_BLOB_MAX, GROW_VTOL_FACTOR+RESIDUAL_SIGMAS, the PATCH_* family, MERGE_MAX_SMALL, MIN_*_FACES, and finally whether the JET still has any job beyond rung ordering). Headline metric: stage-2 constant count, now 37 (`grep -h "^const \|^pub(crate) const \|^pub(super) const " src/stage2/*.rs | wc -l`) — report it whenever it moves.** Seam DETECTION proper is explicitly NOT recommended (curvature-based measured dead 2026-07-20; structural signals are provenance-dependent and near-absent on OCCT Delaunay, so it'd fall back to competition and retire no parameter).

- **MESH PROVENANCE RESEARCH — WHY THE SEAM ASSUMPTION HOLDS, AND THE OPENSCAD GAP (2026-07-28, Opus 5, commit 5a2257f, docs only).** Now in DEVELOPMENT_PLAN under "### Mesh provenance and the seam assumption" (after ## Assumptions), plus a TODO item. **WHY DESIGN PRINCIPLE #2 HOLDS: every B-rep faceter is EDGE-FIRST — discretize each edge ONCE (shared by both adjacent faces), then fill each face's parametric domain out to those fixed boundary polylines. That's how WATERTIGHTNESS is guaranteed, so it is architectural, not a vendor style choice. A tangent edge is an edge in the TOPOLOGY, so it's discretized like any other and no triangle can straddle it.** OCCT docs say it outright; ACIS "maintains edge consistency between adjacent faces"; Parasolid has vertex-matching machinery for it. **KERNELS/FACETERS: Fusion 360 + Inventor → ShapeManager (Autodesk ACIS fork), quad-tree gridding. SolidWorks/Onshape/Solid Edge/Shapr3D/Plasticity → Parasolid. FreeCAD → OpenCASCADE. Rhino → own kernel, regular quad grid + recursive subdivision. Tinkercad/SketchUp/Blender/OpenSCAD → mesh-native, NO B-rep.** (A popular blog claims Fusion uses Parasolid server-side — WRONG, Wikipedia's kernel table says ShapeManager.) **DELAUNAY DOES OCCUR: OCCT's `BRepMesh` is genuinely Delaunay (Watson algorithm) in UV space — that's FreeCAD's mesher AND brepper's own. The assumption SURVIVES because it's a CONSTRAINED Delaunay whose constraints are the pre-discretized face boundaries.** What differs between faceters is the INTERIOR pattern: grid faceters (ACIS/Rhino/Parasolid) leave strong anisotropy, Delaunay much less. **So boundary preservation is dependable everywhere; sampling density/anisotropy is CORROBORATING ONLY — remember this before reaching for tessellation layout as a signal.** Our corpus is Onshape-heavy so NOT representative of the real faceter mix. **OPENSCAD GAP (deliberately unhandled, documented not fixed): no B-rep at any stage (`cylinder()` IS a prism, CSG via CGAL/Manifold). Seam assumption holds TRIVIALLY AND USELESSLY — every facet planar so no triangle spans an orientation change, but EVERY edge is a facet boundary so nothing distinguishes an intended face boundary from a discretization step; only the curvature jump does. No tangencies: C¹ intent becomes a finite dihedral of one step (≥12° on defaults). Fragment rule `n = ceil(max(min(360/$fa, r*2*PI/$fs), 5))`, defaults $fa=12/$fs=2, n caps at 30. OUR `--angular-tolerance` (default 25°) IS THE SILENT ARBITER: $fn ≥ 15 reads smooth → analytic fit; $fn ≤ 14 reads sharp → stays a prism (on defaults that's radius ≳ 4.5mm). $fn=6 hex nut correct by accident. Fits succeed easily where attempted because OpenSCAD INSCRIBES its polygons (why holes print undersized) so vertices lie EXACTLY on the ideal surface → vertex validation ~0 residual; the BINDING check is the centroid/`--surface-tolerance` one, sagitta r(1−cos(180/n)): 0.11mm at r=20,n=30 passes but 0.55mm at r=100,n=30 FAILS → per-facet fallback. THE AMBIGUITY IS GENUINE — a 15-gon prism and a coarse cylinder are the same mesh — so it's a POLICY choice, arguing for an explicit flag over silent auto-detection. Detection signals if needed: constant dihedral of exactly 360/n all the way around a revolution (B-rep steps vary + density changes at seams), and vertices exactly on the circumscribed circle.**

- **MDL/COMPLEXITY-PRICING IDEA REFUTED; INERT GUARD DELETED (2026-07-28, Opus 5).** Suite 502/3-ignored, clippy clean, every probe model decomposes identically. Attempted TODO #2 (replace 5 guards with one complexity-priced model-selection score). **THE PREMISE FAILS — DO NOT RETRY AS STATED.** (1) **ARITHMETIC: coverage pricing cannot reach the target case.** The degenerate torus on stepped_block covers 180 faces where the cylinder it displaces covers 94 — 86 extra faces for 2 extra DOF. Any price rejecting it must charge >~43 faces/DOF, which would also forbid a cylinder ever beating a plane by <86 faces. **The degenerate fit genuinely IS the better description of its OWN support; MDL is right and unhelpful. The fault is that the support is STOLEN from a neighbour, which greedy per-seed selection cannot see.** (2) **The pile had already dissolved.** Per-guard ablation (per-model TYPE breakdowns + full suite): `radius_plausible` **completely inert on 14 models → DELETED** with `hyp_radius`/`conical_local_radius` (−50 lines); `MIN/MAX_HALF_ANGLE_DEG` inert on the corpus but KEPT as VALIDITY guards (a near-zero-half-angle cone has its apex at infinity, can't export); `MAX_TORUS_RADIUS_RATIO` still fires (medium 18→19 tori, filleted_pipe 4→5); **`MIN_AXIAL_SWEEP_DEG` firmly load-bearing (off: stepped_block fine 16/37/18 planes/cyl/tori → 12/38/21).** **NEW FRAMING IN DEVELOPMENT_PLAN — keep these three kinds of guard distinct: IDENTIFIABILITY (does the data pin this parameter at all), VALIDITY (can this be represented/exported), MODEL SELECTION (which candidate wins — the only one that should be a comparison, and the one most often mis-written as a threshold). A fit with an unpinned parameter is NOT a slightly-worse explanation but a free variable that landed somewhere, so NO residual/coverage comparison can detect it.** **METHODOLOGY (important): surface COUNT is too coarse — the first ablation pass showed stepped_block fine unchanged at 71 while its TYPES moved (3 spurious tori). `tmp/probe.sh` prints per-model plane/cyl/cone/sphere/torus/bspline across 14 models = the cheap first check for any stage-2 change; full suite decisive.**

- **SELF-CALIBRATING ACCEPTANCE TOLERANCE (2026-07-27, Opus 5). USER PUSHED BACK: "you're tweaking parameters and adding patches rather than making the fundamental algorithm more robust."** I largely agreed — stage 2 carries **~25 behavioural thresholds** and FOUR UNRELATED KNOBS each sit one step from total collapse on stepped_block medium. **Note the recurrence: the 2026-07-17 pivot was called precisely because development had become "heuristics-on-heuristics", and the same crust re-grew on the NEW architecture within a fortnight. Watch for this deliberately.** Useful split: the INVARIANT fixes (stale-fit refits, area-weighted plane offset, jet-as-prior) are what actually made stepped_block fine pass; the THRESHOLD additions (PATCH_RADII, MIN_AXIAL_SWEEP_DEG) only removed spurious surfaces and did NOT close the compare. **IMPLEMENTED `acceptance_tol` (compete.rs).** The flood bar is now `clamp(RESIDUAL_SIGMAS × median residual of the fit over its own support, vertex_tolerance, GROW_VTOL_FACTOR × vertex_tolerance)`. **RATIONALE: a mesh vertex lies on the true CAD surface to ~1e-6 mm (measured; coarse too), so a face that BELONGS misses its fit by the FIT's error alone, while a face that does NOT belong misses by a geometric departure (~h²/2r one row past a tangent junction). Different quantities — no single absolute bar can straddle that.** MEDIAN not RMS: the outliers are IN the sample. **PLATEAU: identical corpus decompositions at RESIDUAL_SIGMAS 5/8/15 — contrast the absolute bar going correct→collapse over 3.3×.** **HONEST ACCOUNTING: this did NOT remove a constant** — GROW_VTOL_FACTOR demoted to a LOAD-BEARING ceiling and RESIDUAL_SIGMAS added. **ALSO: converted all competition tests to `selected_counts()` — repair can EMPTY a hypothesis without removing it from its vector. Project policy = assert on SELECTED surfaces; this trap fires whenever repair merges anything.**

- **FACET-SCALED GROWTH TOLERANCE LEAD REFUTED; SEED PATCH FIXED INSTEAD (2026-07-27, Opus 5).** **DEAD END, DO NOT RETRY: scaling the flood tolerance with facet size.** Measured on stepped_block medium — `GROW_VTOL_FACTOR` ×1 → 112 surfaces, **×3.3 → COLLAPSE to 2615 per-facet**, ×10 → 90 surfaces w/ spurious sphere. Non-monotonic. **THE ACTUAL FIX: `seed_patch` was GATING its BFS on jet similarity, and on a small fillet tessellated into a few facets the jet is garbage — adjacent facets of one EXACT r=0.5 arc read κ1 = 0.023, 0.162, 2.2. Filter admitted 2–4 faces → can't determine a 5-DOF cylinder → arc becomes one plane per facet.** Now falls back to the plain topological neighbourhood when the filtered patch < MIN_CONDITION_FACES. **GENERAL RULE in DEVELOPMENT_PLAN: wherever the jet is consulted, ask what happens when it's WRONG — the answer must never be "no candidate at all".** **`LEFTOVER_BLOB_MAX`'s whole-region discard is a CLIFF that makes the coarse path untunable.** Medium sits at largest-blob 5 of 8, and EVERY knob tried pushes it over and nukes all 2615 faces to per-facet. **So several constants LOOK load-bearing at their current values when they're only on the safe side of the cliff.** It earned its place (rounded_cube coarse: 43 BRepCheck failures → 0), so measure stage-3/4 VALIDITY directly when changing it.

- **STEPPED_BLOCK FINE PASSES `--compare` (2026-07-26, Opus 5).** `onshape_stepped_block_toroidal_fine_stage41_compare` passing — 51K triangles, 26122 fused faces, **exactly the reference's 71 surfaces (37 cyl + 18 tori + 16 planes)**. **THE ROOT CAUSE WAS STALE FITS, NOT A BAD FITTER.** A fit is only a statement about the faces it was computed from, and the faces a later step removes are exactly the junction rows that pulled it off true. Two sites fixed: **(1) `resolve_claims` (compete.rs)** trims a hypothesis to the faces it won but kept the fit from everything its flood reached → new `refit_in_place`. rounded_cube's r=2 fillets went 1.9976–1.9984 → **2.0000 at 1.6e-6** (1000×). **(2) `relabel_boundaries` (repair.rs)** iterated but only rebuilt face LISTS, never refitting (`rebuild_all` does not refit!) → now refits every surface a move touched, each round. Relabel went **0 → 105 moves**, surfaces 73 → exactly 71. **KEY INSIGHT: boundary repair and fitting are ONE problem.** **THE UNBLOCKER: `fit_plane` (planar.rs) area-weighted its NORMAL but took a plain vertex MEAN for the OFFSET.** The end cap is a few large flat quads ringed by many tiny fillet-first-row quads 9.5e-4 mm off, carrying ~1% of area but HALF the vertices → plane landed at the MIDPOINT **with a FLAT residual distribution and no outlier group for relabel to see**. Area weighting is the correct discretization of ∫(n·x−d)²dA. **DOWNSTREAM AMPLIFICATION: a plane offset δ moves its intersection with a torus it should graze TANGENTIALLY by √(2rδ)** — 4.7e-4 became 4.4e-2 mm. **DIAGNOSTIC LESSON: per-FACE max residual HID bimodality; per-VERTEX quantiles (p10/med/max) exposed it.** The fitter was the obvious suspect and was INNOCENT — unit test `cylinder_fit_exact_on_partial_arc` proves a single row of quads across 90° recovers r to 1e-9; only suspect the fitter again if that fails.

- **`MIN_AXIAL_SWEEP_DEG` AND THE DEGENERATE-SUPER-MODEL MECHANISM (2026-07-26, Opus 5).** **MECHANISM (the general lesson): each analytic type contains the next simpler one as a limit (torus R→∞ = cylinder, cylinder r→∞ = plane), so a patch sweeping only a few degrees about its own axis never observes the revolution and its radius is FREE — and a free parameter produces a WINNING fit, not a useless one, because the richer model covers everything the simpler surface covers and then floods one more tessellation row past the tangent junction on the slack, carrying the largest-validated-area contest.** Populations don't overlap: real tori sweep 84.7–103.4°, impostors 3.1–6.7°; real cylinders ≥64.8°, impostors 2.8–2.9°. True cones exempt (apex pins the axis). **MEASURED DEAD END: sagitta-vs-tolerance does NOT separate them** — the ANGLE matters, not the sag. **Other measured dead ends: a "degenerate_limit" test NEVER FIRES once the sweep guard exists; a post-grow tightening pass at strict vertex tolerance FAILS (115 of 116 grows lose their own start face); raising `MERGE_MAX_SMALL` 16→200 changes nothing.** PERF GOTCHA: `torus_candidate` retrying its other flood starts after a shape-rejected grow made stage 2.3 go 12 s → >600 s; it now returns on the first successful grow. **DIAGNOSTICS in `dump_regions`: `surfaces`, `at <x> <y> <z> [r]`, `owner <face>...`, and (2026-07-28) `locus [a b]` — use them first.**

- **PER-FACET COLLAPSE FIXED (2026-07-25, Opus 5, commit 2fcf888).** **CORRECTION to an earlier note: competition was NOT committing "0 analytic surfaces" — it committed 100 hyps and left only 138/2615 leftover faces; the whole result was then thrown away by the `LEFTOVER_BLOB_MAX` whole-region discard in classify.rs. Always print the pre-discard counts before diagnosing.** Three measured bugs: **(1) degenerate cone on a thin support** — an r=0.5 fillet band ONE QUAD TALL barely constrains the axis via vertex residual, so `fit_cone`'s LM tips the axis INTO the band's own plane: bogus 12.2° cone at 3.8e-7 mm beat the correct r=0.5003 cylinder at 3.9e-6 mm. Fix `axis_normals_consistent` (conical.rs): on a cone `|n·axis| = sin(half_angle)` holds identically, so **the normals pin the axis exactly where positions are degenerate**; accepted only if the support's MEDIAN facet satisfies it. **(2) seed patch bounded in hops, not space** — `MAX_PATCH_DEPTH`=3 walks ~18 mm along a fillet chain swept with 6 mm facets; new `PATCH_RADII`=4 caps the patch at 4 radii of curvature of the seed's own jet. **ABLATION: with PATCH_RADII=∞ the medium mesh collapses back to 2615 per-facet planes — load-bearing.** **(3) freeform fitted a DISCONNECTED face set** — when islands are not absorbed the code fitted the leftovers alone, but the islands may have been the only thing connecting them; **this is the whole explanation of the `closed band: no path between boundary loops` error.**

- **OCCT 8.0.0_p1 upgrade LANDED (2026-07-22, commit 4b20904).** Ported `opencascade-sys` usage across 5 files: `t_colgp::/t_col_std::X` → `n_collection::`; handles → `geom::Handle_Geom_*`/`geom2d::Handle_Geom2d_*`; shortened ctor/method suffixes. **GOTCHA: rustc's fuzzy "similar name" suggestions repeatedly point at the wrong (rational, weighted) BSpline ctor overload — always verify the arg list; the non-rational ones are `new_arr1_pnt2d_arr1_double_arr1_int_int_bool` (5-arg), `new_arr1_pnt_arr1_double_arr1_int_int` (4-arg), `new_arr2_pnt_arr1_double2_arr1_int2_int2` (7-arg surface).** Zero regressions. **The partial-torus STEP round-trip issue is FIXED by 8.0.0_p1.** OCCT 8.0's STEP reader also LOADS the 4 ccad reference STEPs that gave "Reterror" under 7.9.3. **Build mechanics**: brepper keeps its OWN builtin OCCT under `brepper/target/OCCT`; occt-sys now writes a `.occt-src-stamp` and rebuilds on mismatch. For THIS working tree, `brepper/target/OCCT` is a SYMLINK to `../opencascade-rs/target/OCCT`, per the user's instruction to share one build rather than duplicate the ~15-min compile.

- **STAGE-2 REWRITE COMPLETE — all 7 roadmap steps (2026-07-20 → 2026-07-24).** Partition-then-classify: 2.1 sharp edges + jet prior → 2.2 regions → 2.3 whole-region DOF ladder / scoped competition / torus promotion / freeform leftovers → 2.4 repair → 2.5 output. Old BFS pipeline, `MeshFace` hypothesis fields and `--skip-stage` all DELETED (step 7b, −3860/+37 lines). **Clean cascade: the fitters' `fit_surface` path never touched MeshFace fields or BFS-only methods — all such refs lived in generate_seeds/acceptance_test.** Later fixes: **hole → sphere FIXED 2026-07-23** (a short cylinder tessellated as a few rings is vertex-ambiguous with a sphere — two same-radius rings at ±h lie exactly on a sphere of √(R²+h²) — so `region_is_developable` uses the jet's min/max principal-curvature ratio to try the conical rung first; ordering only). **Fillet over-segmentation FIXED 2026-07-24** via four fixes: **magnitude-ordered principal curvatures** (`a1=max(|k1|,|k2|)` — the jet orders by SIGNED value so a concave hole wall's dominant curvature lands in k2 and gating on |k1| read the deep hole as FLAT); seed-patch conditioning (`condition_patch`); DOF-aware `resolve_claims` (a face several surfaces explain within vtol goes to the SIMPLEST); fat-torus rejection `r ≤ 0.7·R`. **l_bracket FIXED 2026-07-24**: a full cylindrical hole's area-weighted plane normal cancels to NaN and the ladder VACUOUSLY validated it (`dist>vtol` is false for NaN) → `PlanarSurface::fit_surface` now returns None on a non-finite fit. **LESSON: run `cargo test -- --ignored` before assuming any blocker is still live** — 7 of 17 disabled tests already passed. **Remaining 3 ignores: cone_tangent_planes, cone_15x20 (faceted reference STEP, unfixable without new reference data), stepped_block_toroidal medium.**

- **STAGE 2 ARCHITECTURE PIVOT (2026-07-17 plan, 2026-07-20 resolution).** User concluded development had become heuristics-on-heuristics and asked for a back-to-basics restructure. **The 2026-07-20 verdict that decided it, from measurement (dump_regions): the curvature-jump seam classifier's FALSE POSITIVES ARE A CLASS, NOT A BUG — nosecone's cone showed 129 fragments whose descriptors form a CORRECT continuous gradient (1/ρ(h)), while pipe_elbow's REAL torus↔cylinder junction reads 0.33 BELOW the 0.35 fire threshold that the cone's gradient steps exceed. The two classes OVERLAP in the decision statistic — no threshold exists.** Hysteresis hit its 10-round cap (oscillating) on every fragmenting model. Pure cones segment perfectly via sharp edges alone. **So: keep sharp-edge partition, demote the jet to a PRIOR, and move junction discovery to FIT VALIDATION.** Contingency dissolved into §2.3; curvature-jump classifier autopsied in Abandoned Approaches. **Design principle #2 (tessellators seam B-rep boundaries even at tangencies) is the anchor the whole architecture rests on — see the 2026-07-28 provenance research above for why it is architectural rather than incidental.**

## 🤖 Assistant
<!-- msg_011CdUzx8WeZgv7kvXbMqx9k -->

All four steps are done and committed as `47a2acf` (the three untracked `tests/downloaded/` files were already there before I started; I left them alone).

## What landed

**`src/stage2/locus.rs`** — the junction geometry. The core decision: at a tangent junction, never intersect. A plane offset by δ from the torus it should graze meets it in a circle displaced by √(2rδ) — that's the recorded 4.7e-4 → 4.4e-2 mm amplification. The minimum-distance locus doesn't depend on that offset at all; sliding the plane along its own normal leaves the tangency circle exactly where it was. There's a unit test that asserts this rather than just the comment claiming it.

Most tangent junctions are coaxial surfaces of revolution, so I reduced each pair to the meridian half-plane, where every surface is a line or a circle and the side test is displacement along the common tangent. That subsumes the per-type cases and shows why neither ρ nor h works alone — at each junction one of them is stationary. Two junctions aren't axisymmetric and are built directly: a plane grazing a cylinder along a ruling, and the tube junction where a straight fillet continues into a corner fillet (perpendicular axes, junction at the foot of the perpendicular from the torus centre onto the cylinder axis). That last one was 36 of the pairs on stepped_block fine and would be invisible to a coaxial-only implementation.

**`snap_boundaries`** in repair.rs, running after relabel and merge. **Loud, as you asked** — a tangent pair with no locus, or one that fails validation, is reported on stderr and left alone. That paid for itself: it caught a real bug on `filleted_cylinder`, where a disc triangulated with every vertex on its rim has all its corners *on* the junction and on both surfaces at once, so the corners decide nothing and the face interior has to.

**Retired relabel's 2× anti-flapping margin and its 10-round cap.** Both were the price of comparing a face against two fits each contaminated by that face; in-loop refitting removed the contamination, and letting each face move at most once bounds the loop as a convergence statement rather than a number.

Suite 509 passing / 3 ignored, clippy clean, corpus decomposition byte-identical.

## Three things you should weigh

**Snap is inert on the corpus.** With the margin and cap gone, unconstrained relabel reaches the same boundary on its own — snap moves zero faces on every model, and the suite passes with snap ablated. I measured that deliberately rather than assuming. It's a correctness guarantee, not a fix. I kept it (item C needs exactly this per-face criterion, and the diagnostic depends on the module) but it's your call whether an inert pass earns its place; it's flagged in TODO.

**The validation target didn't move, and I know why.** stepped_block fine still reports 4.36e-2 mm, medium still fails at 1.56e-1. Neither is a wrong-side-face problem: fine's worst edge is a torus↔cylinder tube junction whose boundary sits on the locus to ~1e-6 mm (4.363e-2 is exactly one tessellation row of the r=1 torus at 2.5°), and medium's is torus↔B-spline — the known decomposition gap. So item A was never going to move those numbers.

**The constant count went 35 → 37,** not down. The margin and cap were inline literals; `locus.rs` adds `COAXIAL_TOL` and `TANGENCY_TOL_RAD` (which gives stage 3.2's existing tangency criterion one shared definition across stages). `MERGE_MAX_SMALL` is untested — it's a merge criterion, not a boundary one.

The lasting instruments are probably worth more than the pass: `dump_regions locus [a b]`, stage 3.1's compare now naming its worst edge by both surfaces even on success, and `boundaries_sit_on_their_junctions`, which pins zero wrong-side faces across seven models — much sharper than a surface count, which a one-row boundary error leaves completely intact.
